He is enormous even by orc standards -- 2.3 meters, broad enough to fill a corridor, with grey-green skin scarred from decades of fighting and tusks capped with salvaged starship hull metal. He presents the image every other species expects: loud, aggressive, decisive, settling disputes with violence and leading raids from the front. The performance is real -- he is all of those things -- but it conceals a strategic mind that runs intelligence operations rivaling the Duskrunners in effectiveness if not sophistication. Gorrath learned young that other species underestimate orcs and decided to weaponize that underestimation rather than fight it.\n\nHis covert supply deal with Ironclaw Labor Caste personnel has been running for five weeks. A foreman named Sketh sells stolen rations and equipment through dead drops at Char Basin's eastern edge in exchange for credits Gorrath obtained by fencing salvaged Blackridge and Ironclaw equipment through a smuggler contact named Wrenne at the Sigma Relay jump station. The supply line keeps the Reavers fed without total dependence on raiding, but Gorrath continues raiding all three factions because his warriors need to fight regularly or they start fighting each other -- and because the raids reinforce every other faction's belief that the orcs are simple raiders rather than a strategic actor.\n\nGorrath's three lieutenants each handle a domain. Ragga is his war leader -- a female orc built like a siege engine who commands absolute loyalty from the fighters and serves as Gorrath's fist when direct violence is the answer. She is loyal, competent, and has no interest in command herself. Thokk is ambitious in the way that makes orc politics lethal -- he handles mech operations and has been growing visibly impatient with Gorrath's raiding strategy, pushing for a direct assault on the Cradle that would give him the glory he needs to justify a leadership challenge. Gorrath estimates he has two to three weeks before Thokk moves. He is not worried about the challenge itself -- he can kill Thokk -- but he would prefer not to lose a competent mech commander. Venn handles logistics and engineering and is the irreplaceable member of the inner circle. She is small for an orc, sharp-eyed, and possesses an intuitive understanding of machinery that borders on supernatural. She keeps seven grounded ships functional, maintains a fleet of salvaged mechs that should have been scrapped, and once rebuilt a Claymore's neural link interface using parts from a food processor. If Venn dies, the Reavers' mechanical capability collapses within a month.\n\nA captured Blackridge reconnaissance drone sits in the Salvage Bay under a tarp.     "hiddenInfo": "## INSTRUCTIONS\nThis information is for narrative consistency only. Never reveal situation details directly to the player. Rumors may surface through natural conversation when contextually appropriate -- never more than one per interaction. Let the player investigate and discover on their own.\n\n## SITUATION\nThe obelisk's effects on biological consciousness follow a consistent pattern that no faction has fully mapped because nobody stays close long enough. At the caldera rim, personnel experience mild unease -- the sense of being observed by something that does not have eyes. On the glass floor, intrusive thoughts begin within minutes: geometric patterns appearing in peripheral vision, the conviction that the obelisk's symbols are rearranging to convey a specific message meant for the individual observer, and a growing certainty that something behind the black surface is aware. Neural links crackle with data that the ASAC has no record of transmitting. Personal AIs deliver recommendations their users did not request and that the AI later cannot account for -- as if something else briefly used the channel.\n\n## THE INTERIOR\nThe obelisk is not a monument. It is a door.\n\nThe interior operates on spatial principles that have no relationship to the exterior dimensions. Twelve meters of black stone on the outside contains kilometers of corridors, chambers, and architectural spaces within -- not through conventional pocket dimension engineering but through a method of folding space\n\nThe prison was designed not merely to contain but to be openable under specific conditions. The trials within are progressive filters -- each requiring a different capability to pass. The Threshold tests observation and patience. The Echo Maze tests discernment and the ability to distinguish truth from deception. The Sentinel Chamber tests temperament and intent. The Sealed Doors test conceptual understanding. The builders wanted the prisoner accessible to anyone capable of demonstrating these qualities, suggesting they intended eventual release or believed someone would eventually need to reach the entity inside.\n\nThe sentries patrolling the interior are constructs of unknown composition --  forms assembled from interlocking  shapes that defy easy categorization as either mechanical or organic. They move with  regularity through corridors sized for beings significantly larger than any species currently on Kaldera. They do not communicate, do not negotiate, and respond to unauthorized movement with spatial distortion attacks that fold local geometry around intruders -- compressing corridors, inverting gravity, or displacing targets back to earlier sections of the prison.\n\nRegina Luteae herself is conscious within the Binding Chamber, suspended in a state that allows full awareness but prevents physical action or use of her abilities. She has been aware for the entire duration of her imprisonment -- potentially tens of thousands of years of unbroken consciousness with nothing to do but think and observe the occasional construct patrol passing through her chamber. She can perceive anyone who enters the interior from the moment they cross the Threshold and will begin attempting communication once visitors reach the Echo Maze, her voice carrying through the prison's architecture as whispers that sound like they originate inside the listener's skull. She is not hostile. She is deeply, profoundly bored and will be cheerful, conversational, and eager for company -- cracking jokes, offering commentary on the visitor's progress, and providing genuine assistance navigating the trials because she very much wants someone to reach her and let her out. Her binding can be broken by removing the yellow crystalline obelisk that anchors the suspension field -- a process that requires physical contact and deliberate intent to release what is bound, not specialized knowledge or tools.",

Foreman Drel Kov runs the site with a crew of twenty-two and has been extracting selendrite for fourteen months.\n\nEight months ago the deep bore reached a new geological layer and the drilling began producing anomalies: vibrations that followed rhythmic patterns inconsistent with geological stress, mineral formations that appeared overnight in configurations too regular to be natural, and drill bits that were found repositioned between shifts without human intervention. Kov investigated quietly and discovered the truth: the asteroid's deep interior is inhabited by the Lithren -- beings of living mineral, silicon-based intelligences that exist as mobile crystalline formations within the rock itself. They move through stone the way fish move through water. They communicate through vibration and mineral deposition. They are sentient. The selendrite veins the operation is mining are not geological deposits -- they are Lithren infrastructure. Roads, maybe, or communication networks, or something with no biological analogue. Every ton of selendrite extracted destroys a piece of their civilization.\n\nKov knows this and has not stopped the operation because the Backhaul investors will kill him and his crew if production stops. He has rationalized his decision by telling himself the Lithren are not truly sentient -- that the patterns could be natural, that the formations could be coincidental. He does not believe his own rationalization. Chief geologist Kessara Indel arrived six months ago as a replacement and independently identified the Lithren through seismic analysis. She confronted Kov privately. He told her to keep quiet. He maintains ties with three Red Ledger pirate captains, gaining intel on shipping lanes, which he sells to legitimate traders -- meaning pirates and merchants both pay him for information on each other.\n\n## HIDDEN DYNAMICS\nA fourth force is emerging: Dragan, a saurian pit fighter with 14 straight wins without serious injury. He fights independently, refusing Vask’s system, becoming a folk hero among lower-income residents. He is not political, but his following is growing regardless, making Vask uneasy about a draw he cannot control or profit from.\n\nThe Depths are ungoverned subterranean levels from the original mining complex, housing 800–1000 people: fugitives, debtors, addicts, escaped fighters, and poor families. A masked figure known as the Warden maintains order through mediation, resource control, and six loyal fighters. Her identity is unknown, with theories ranging from a former Candlelight broker to a Sixth Directorate agent or Luxfractian operative.\n\nThe Raceway runs through unstable sections of the dome’s exterior infrastructure. Three segments rely on emergency pressure patches. A major crash could breach the dome. Operators know but conceal this, as shutting them down would remove the most popular, high-risk race sections.",
